`<backup>` is a folder of `build/save-backup` or of the profile's `save-backup` (the game keeps its last three saves
there), or a save folder or `.zip` from elsewhere, such as a crash report's zipped profile folder; `--script` defaults
to `tools/input/load_save.txt` (title screen to the first save slot), and a recording made with
`NX2SARIA_INPUT_RECORD` replays the same way. `--wall` ends a run whose script never reaches the world clock;
`NX2SARIA_SHOT_EVERY` takes its pictures by wall time.

`--boot-map <area>[:<start position>]` starts the game in that area from the save without the title screen or the file
menu (`runtime/patches/boot_map.c`), with `--boot-map-slot`, `--boot-map-age` and `--boot-map-time` beside it. Its
input script defaults to `tools/input/boot_map.txt`, which presses nothing. The run fails (exit code 3) when the log
has no "boot map: arrived in" line for the area asked for; the boot map's lines are printed at the end.

```
python3 tools/run_game.py <game folder> --save <backup> --boot-map HyruleField_Child:HyruleField_FrontOfMarket \
    --boot-map-time 21:00 --seconds 10 --wall 180 --tag field-night
```

- The import of `launcher/saves.py` carries `# noqa: E402` with its reason (Import Saves' search, for a save from
  elsewhere), which stays.
- `LOCK_OFFSET`: the Windows byte-range lock sits past the holder text, so other processes can still read it.
- `MAX_RUNS` (3, or `NX2SARIA_MAX_RUNS`): more parallel runs exhaust device memory (16 GB RX 9070).
- `GAME_EXES`: the game's executable as each tool names it: `tools/build_game.py`, `tools/make_game.py` on Windows,
  `tools/make_game.py` on Linux, `tools/build_game.py` on Linux.

### `count()`

Running processes of this executable name. On Linux it reads `/proc/<pid>/comm`, the process name cut to 15
characters; a process that ended while the list was read is skipped.

### `keep_in_background()`

Pushes the game window behind the user's windows as soon as it appears, for builds older than
`NX2SARIA_BACKGROUND_WINDOW`: `SetWindowPos` with `HWND_BOTTOM` and `NOSIZE | NOMOVE | NOACTIVATE`.

### `take_slot()`, `try_lock()`

`take_slot()` blocks until one of `MAX_RUNS` run slots is free and holds it (a locked file) until this process exits.
`try_lock()` takes an exclusive OS lock on the open file without blocking; false if another process holds it.

### `take_gpu_lock()`

Blocks until this process holds the machine-wide measured-run lock (`build/.gpu-run.lock`) and returns the open file.
The OS drops the lock when the process exits or dies, so a stale file never blocks. The file text names the holder
(pid and build path) for the waiters.

### `save_source()`

Folder, slot and names to leave out, of the save to run. A backup named in `build/save-backup` or the profile's
`save-backup` is a whole save: a bare name is a backup's name, a path is searched. A folder or `.zip` from elsewhere
(a crash report's zipped profile folder, a game folder, an emulator's save) is searched as the launcher's Import Saves
searches it; `slot` names the one slot it holds, `None` for a whole save. `zipfile` leaves out absolute paths and `..`
parts when the zip is unpacked.

### `profile_copy()`

`save-tx` is removed from the copy: a finished or interrupted commit there would be loaded in place of the save
swapped in (`runtime/hle/fs_host.c`).

### `boot_map_report()`

Prints the boot map's lines of the run's log; returns 3 in place of a clean exit when Link did not arrive in the area
asked for (`runtime/patches/boot_map.c` `boot_map_tick()` writes the line).

### `main()`

Without `--show` on Windows the game gets `NX2SARIA_BACKGROUND_WINDOW=1`: SDL windows open in front either way.

## `tools/runtime_folders.py`

The runtime's files by subsystem folder: which file of `runtime/` moved from the top level into which folder, and the
script that made the move and rewrote the paths that name the files ([architecture.md](../architecture.md)).

```
python tools/runtime_folders.py move              # git mv every file of FOLDERS from runtime/ into its folder
python tools/runtime_folders.py rewrite [FILE...]  # runtime/<name> -> runtime/<folder>/<name> in those files
python tools/runtime_folders.py where NAME...      # the path below runtime/ a file has now
python tools/runtime_folders.py table              # the table as Markdown
```

`rewrite` without files takes every tracked file outside `runtime/`, `tools/xlate/` and `tools/vendor/` that names one
(git grep) and changes `runtime/<name>` and `runtime\<name>` in any text, and in Python a path joined from a runtime
folder, such as `ROOT / 'runtime' / '<name>'` or `rt / '<name>'`. A branch made before the move takes a rebase as
usual (git follows the renames); a file it adds that names a moved one is brought up to date with `rewrite <file>`,
and a C file it adds at the top of `runtime/` still builds there, as the top level is compiled as before.

The files that stay at the top of `runtime/`: the entry point, the guest's core (`cpu.h`, `simd.h`, `simd_fp.h`,
`dispatch.h`, `host.h`: the lifted C includes them, and its compile has only `runtime/` on its path), the loader,
guest memory, the log, `cfg.h` (`runtime/platform/win32.c` includes it as `../cfg.h`), the small shared helpers and
the Windows resource files.

- `VK_EXTRA`: the rest of `vk_*`, and the headers of the Vulkan back end that carry another name. `FOLDERS['vk']` is
  `vk/` as it was on the day of the move (the `vk_*` files then); a `vk_*` file added later stays where it is put.
- `TEXT` matches `runtime/<name>` and `runtime\<name>` as a whole name (not `vk_fsr.c` inside `vk_fsr.cpp`; a full
  stop after it may end a sentence). `JOIN` matches a Python path join that ends in a
  moved name, from a runtime folder: `... / 'runtime' / 'name'`, `rt / 'name'`.

### `move()`

git mv of each file of `FOLDERS` from the top of `runtime/` into its folder.

### `rewrite()`

The translator's sources are left as they are (`tools/build_xlate.py` hashes them into the translator's identity), as
is the vendored code.

## `tools/sdl3_pin.py`

SDL3 for a Linux build on a system that has none: the headers and `libSDL3.so.0` a build kit carries
([README-BuildKit.md](../README-BuildKit.md)). Standard library only: the build kit's Python runs this.

```
python tools/sdl3_pin.py [--dest toolchain/sdl3] [--deb FILE FILE | --sysroot FILE]
```

The runtime's window, input and audio are SDL 3. Ubuntu 24.04 LTS and the distributions built on it (Linux Mint 22 and
others on the noble base) package no SDL3 at all, so a build with the system's compiler there found no `SDL3/SDL.h`
(issue #11). This is the SDL3 a kit carries for them, in `toolchain/sdl3`. `tools/make_game.py` takes it where the
compiler finds no SDL3 headers of the system's (`sdl3_choice()`): the runtime is compiled and the game linked against
it, `libSDL3.so.0` goes beside the game (`place_sdl3()`), and the preflight runs a program linked against it, so that
a library it needs and the system lacks stops the build, by name, before the long steps.

It is SDL 3.4.18 as the Steam Runtime 3 builds it, the build the Steam Runtime 3 "sniper" SDK sysroot holds. Built for
the Steam Runtime's glibc (its newest symbol version is GLIBC_2.29), it runs on any distribution at least that new. It
links the desktop's client libraries directly instead of loading them when it starts: libX11, libXext, libXcursor,
libXi, libXfixes, libXrandr, libXss, libXtst, libxkbcommon, libwayland-client, -cursor and -egl, libdecor-0, libpulse,
libasound and libfribidi, all of which a desktop install of Ubuntu or Mint has.

The files come from one of two places, checked either way:

- the runtime's apt pool: the packages libsdl3-0 (the library) and libsdl3-dev (the headers), 2.7 MB, each checked
  against its SHA-256 (`DEBS`: package to address and SHA-256). The pool keeps the current version of a package only,
  so these go once the Steam Runtime moves on;
- then the dated snapshot of the sniper SDK sysroot that holds the same build (`SYSROOT`: the snapshot with libsdl3-0
  and libsdl3-dev `DEB_VERSION` installed), 1.2 GB, read as it downloads and checked against its SHA-256 at the end,
  nothing of it written to disk but the files below.

Both must give exactly the files `CONTENT_SHA256` names (`content_digest()` of the kept files, `pinned.txt` aside: the
86 headers, the library and the copyright file), so the kit's SDL3 is the same from either.

Kept: `include/SDL3/*.h`, `lib/libSDL3.so.0` (the library's file under its soname), the package's copyright file
(`SDL3-copyright.txt`: SDL is zlib, and the file names the licences of the parts it contains) and `pinned.txt`.

- `VERSION`: SDL's own version, as `SDL_version.h` defines it. `DEB_VERSION`: the packages' version in the Steam
  Runtime 3 apt pool.
- `FOLDER`: below a checkout's or a kit's root: `include/`, `lib/`, the copyright file. `STAMP`: in the folder, the
  packages' version, written last. `LIB`: the soname the game is linked against and finds beside itself.
- `HEADER` and the patterns beside it: members of a package's data archive or of the sysroot that are kept; `HEADER`'s
  file name part takes no `..` and no folder, so nothing is written outside the folder. `NEAR`: files a kept member
  may be a hard link to: SDL3's own, a few MB, held while an archive is read.

### `have()`

True when the folder holds the pinned SDL3, complete (the stamp is written after the last file). A stamp that is not
ASCII (`ValueError`) is no stamp.

### `kept_name()`, `take()`

`kept_name()`: where a member of the packages or the sysroot goes in the folder, `None` when it is not kept. `take()`:
the kept members of an open tar archive into `kept` (folder path to bytes). A kept member that is a hard link to one
of SDL3's files read earlier (`NEAR`) takes that file's bytes; symbolic links are not followed (the library is taken
by its own file name).

### `deb_data()`, `deb_files()`

`deb_data()`: name and bytes of the data archive of a Debian package: an ar archive whose `data.tar.*` member holds
the files. Members start on even offsets. `deb_files()`: the kept files of a Debian package (bytes) into `kept`; the
pinned packages are xz, as the Steam Runtime's dpkg writes them.

### `content_digest()`

One SHA-256 over the kept files' paths and SHA-256s: equal for two sources that gave the same files.

### `clear()`, `write()`, `unpack()`

`clear()` removes what an earlier unpack wrote into the folder, and nothing else of it. `write()` puts the kept files
into the folder, after they are checked: complete and exactly the pinned ones. `unpack()`: the headers, the library
and its copyright file of the two packages (name to bytes) into the folder.

### `HashingReader`, `sysroot_files()`

`HashingReader`: a file object over a download that hashes what is read through it and counts it. `sysroot_files()`:
the kept files of the sysroot archive read from `f` (a download or a file), checked against `SYSROOT`'s SHA-256 once
all of it is read; what follows the archive's end marker is read too, for the hash.

### `download()`

The bytes at `url`, `None` when the server or the network does not give them.

### `resolve()`

The folder holding the pinned SDL3 (default `<root>/toolchain/sdl3`), fetched and unpacked there when it does not hold
it yet. `debs`: the two packages on disk instead of the download, in any order, each one of the pinned files;
`sysroot`: the sniper SDK sysroot archive on disk. Returns the folder: `include/` goes on the include path,
`lib/libSDL3.so.0` into the link.

### `header_version()`

Major, minor and micro as `SDL_version.h` defines them in `text`, `None` when it does not.

## `tools/shader_census.py`

Census of every Maxwell shader in the extracted RomFS and ExeFS, and an offline translation pass. The notice at its
top is that of the code it holds from another project and stays
([THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md](../../THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)): the inventory, the bnsh, sharcb and sarc readers and the
package walk. Unlike where those came from, this pass reads no shared shader list, runs no spirv-cross and makes no
bind-state variants; the module scan reads the NSO files under `exefs/` directly; and a control section must start
with `CONTROL_MAGIC` (`sharcb_shaders()` says why).

It reads `build/extracted/<version>/` (the RomFS image plus `index.json` as `tools/romfs_index.py` reads them, and
`exefs/`) and writes under `build/<version>/shaders/` only:

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `code/<key>.code` | translator input (graphics: SPH and instructions, compute: instructions) |
| `jobs.json` | key to job, source path, origin (`bnsh`, `sharcb`, `ndsr` or `main`) |
| `spv/<key>.spv` | with `--translate` |
| `failures.txt` | with `--translate`, when something failed: groups by message |

The key is `sha256(control[:0x880] + code)`, the shared shader list's key. It needs Python 3.14
(`compression.zstd`), so it runs in `.venv-py314`:

```
.venv-py314\Scripts\python tools\shader_census.py --version v121 --translate
```

- `zstd` is `tools/zstd_compat.py`: `compression.zstd` on 3.14, the zstandard package before.
- `CONTROL_MAGIC`: the NVN program control section magic, u32 0x98761234.
- `NVN2_STAGES`: the stage number in bits 16 and up of control +0x54. `NVN2_CONTROL` (0xe0): the part of an NVN2
  control section the runtime keeps (`runtime/gfx/gfx_shader.c`, its NVN2 part). `NVN2_SLOTS`: program slots that may
  point at a stage record: fragment +40, task +64, mesh +72 seen.
- `SHADER_DIRS`: the RomFS directories that can hold shaders: the agl and gsys packages, the effects, terrain, the UI
  layout archives and the game's own shader packs. Every shader of 1.1.0 (27,576) comes from these six; they are 1,996
  entries and 147 MiB, 0.9% of a RomFS whose every file the walk used to read and decompress first (65 s against
  2.8 s). The Switch 2 game keeps its UI layout shaders (MergeShader) in `Layout2Shader/`.

### `sarc()`

Names only key the members here, nothing is written under them: an odd or repeated one is skipped.

### `nvn2_record()`, `nvn2_stages()`

`nvn2_record()`: control and code of the stage record at `offset` when it is an NVN2 one: `{u64, control offset, code
offset, u32 code size, u32 control size}`, the control shorter than NVN1's 0x880 and starting with the magic. Else
`None`. `nvn2_stages()`: the stages of an NVN2 (Switch 2) program as the runtime keys them: the control section's
first 0xe0 bytes in a zeroed 0x880 one, and the code from its first byte up to the end of its c1 constants (+0x40 to
+0x44, after the code of +0x38 bytes), the c1 part included.

### `bnsh_shaders()`

BNSH absolute offsets are relative to each embedded BNSH, not BFSHA. The SDK utility BNSH uses an older, shorter
control layout and is skipped. A control is cut to 0x880: some are 0x88c; NVN and the runtime's key read 0x880.

### `sharcb_shaders()`

agl shader packages (`.sharcb`, magic BAHS): records `<u64 code offset in the pool, u32 control size (0x880 or 0x88c),
i32 offset of the control from the record>`; a record may reuse an earlier record's control. NVN2 (Switch 2) packages
hold the same records with a 0xe0-byte control section.

The scan finds records by their size word, so it also meets 16-byte groups that only look like one. Without the magic
check, 1.2.1 yields 87 such keys: each has control offset 0 (the "control" starts at the record itself, so its first 8
bytes are the code offset) and no `CONTROL_MAGIC`. The game hands NVN real control sections and the runtime key hashes
those, so no draw can ever produce these keys. Their code is cut from the wrong place: they were all 67 translation
failures of 1.2.1 (IndexOutOfRange in ShaderHeader.ctor and ArgumentOutOfRange in Decoder.FillBlock, on 0 to 4,718
bytes of code). A scan without the magic check keeps them; the 7 that translate are V rows in the shared shader list
only because that list's producer folds the seed's own `.variants` files back in. A candidate without `CONTROL_MAGIC`
is skipped and counted in `rejected[0]`.

### `ndsr_shaders()`

NDSR packages (`Lib/agl/agl_ndsr*.pck`: compute programs). Items of 8 bytes, `<u32 offset, u32 size << 4 | kind>`: an
NVN2 control section is kind 6 with size 0xe0 and the item after it, kind 7, is its code.

### `nvn2_sharcb_record()`

Key, stage, control and code of an NVN2 `.sharcb` record, as `nvn2_stages()` cuts them, or `None`. A size word of 0xe0
is common, so a candidate counts only with the control magic and a known stage.

### `nso_image()`

The decompressed text, rodata and data segments of an NSO, concatenated (enough to find embedded BNSH files).

### `startup_package()`

The RomFS files the shared shader list's startup shaders come from; effects and UI layouts carry more.

### `inventory()`

Every unique shader: key to `dict(stage, control, code, source, origin, startup)`. As in the shared shader list, a
shader is a startup shader when it comes from an agl package, a game module, or a `startup_package()` RomFS file; the
others come from effect and UI layout resources, which the list covers only for bind states players have met. It also
returns the number of `.sharcb` record candidates rejected for a missing control magic (`sharcb_shaders()`). BFSHA and
BNSH files, effect and layout resources are searched for complete, independently addressed BNSH files.

### `write_shaders()`, `worker()`

`write_shaders()` writes the code files and `jobs.json`; returns key to `dict(job, source, origin)`. `worker()`: one
`--serve` process of the translator for a slice of keys; returns the number translated, failures (key to message) and
errors (key to message).

## `tools/shader_list.py`

The shared shader list, another project's file (`games/<game>/assets/shader_list.tsv` in its checkout under `refs/`;
its licence is in [THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md](../../THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)), as our tools read it: every row of one
revision, plus the V rows of every older revision that it dropped.

The list drops V rows its producer's runtime no longer needs: default variants, which that runtime translates for
every shader (dropped 2026-10-22 to 2026-10-25), and variants that differ only in vertex input or colour output types
(2026-10-26). Our answers (`runtime/gfx/gfx_shader.c`) still tell those apart, so a dropped row names a variant our
play meets: at 1340bee the list alone has 134 rows of 1.4.3 shaders, its history 24,839, and of 1.2.1 6,771 and
25,238. A row no draw asks for costs one translation. No copy of the list goes into the repository.

`TAIL` is the list's path under the checkout's game folder (`list_path()`).

### `list_path()`

The shader list's path at `rev`: the one `games/<game>/assets/shader_list.tsv` of the checkout.

### `history()`

`rev`'s list, then each V row of an older revision that no newer one has, newest first. A shallow clone has `rev`'s
list only: preparing from it skips most variants and shows only as stutter in play, so it stops and names
`tools/fetch_refs.sh`.

## `tools/spin_waits.py`

Finds the spin waits of the ExeFS modules and prints them as `spin` lines of a patch table (`recomp/patches.py`). The
notice at its top is that of the spin wait search, which came with a licence
([THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md](../../THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)): the part of this file that is `Search.rw()`,
`Search.kind()`, `Search.rmw()`, `Search.lock()`, `Search.store()`, `Search.quick()`, `Search.region()` and
`Search.region1()` (in its source `spin_rw`, `spin_kind`, `spin_rmw`, `spin_lock`, `spin_store`, `spin_quick`,
`spin_region`, `spin_region1`, under "Spin waits").

```
spin_waits.py --exefs build/extracted/v143/exefs --patches recomp/patches-v143.txt [--modules main,subsdk0,sdk]
```

A guest loop that polls memory until another thread changes it fills only its own core on the Switch, where a better
thread pinned there preempts it. Here it holds one of the six virtual cores (`runtime/hle/kernel.c`) and a host core,
and when the poll takes and releases a lock on every pass, two pollers hand the lock's cache line between their cores
so that the thread whose item it is rarely wins its exclusive store (a job queue polled this way gave CPU frames of
30-210 ms every 1-10 s). The generator sends the back-edges of such loops through
`runtime/hle/kernel.c` `rt_spin_gate()`, which slows the poll down and hands the virtual core to a waiting thread
once the loop has polled for a while.

A loop counts only when it provably cannot leave by itself. From its head T every path back to T (the region R,
within `SPIN_WIN` bytes after T) runs loads, register and flag arithmetic, direct branches and barriers; no call, SVC,
system register or event wait; nothing live at T is written in R. Allowed besides: an exclusive add of a constant that
every path back to T undoes on the same address, a spin lock taken and released on the same address on every path, a
test loop on a word inside R, and at most `SPIN_STORES` stores of registers R never writes (fixed values to fixed
addresses, so the passes repeat after a few). Sites of the patch table are kept out of regions.

It runs on the development machine (capstone); the table it prints is checked in, so the installer's generator needs
no disassembler. The three imports after the `sys.path` lines carry `# noqa: E402`, which stays.

- `SPIN_WIN` (512): bytes from a backward branch back to its target. `SPIN_TOPS`: the top bytes of b, b.cond, cbz and
  cbnz, tbz and tbnz. `SPIN_PATHS`, `SPIN_STEPS`, `SPIN_STORES`: complete paths back to T; search steps; stores in a
  region.
- `SPIN_STOP`: words that end every path through them by their encoding alone: bl; br, blr and ret; svc and brk;
  stores (immediate, unscaled, pre and post-index, register offset, vector); stp and stnp; LSE atomics. Exclusive
  stores and pre and post-index loads are left to the full check.

### `norm()`

w and x views as `x<n>`, vector views as `v<n>`, zero registers as `None`.

### `Search`

The spin waits of one module image. `touched`: image offsets no region may contain (patch sites).

### `Search.candidates()`

Loop head to the backward branches to it, branches at most `SPIN_WIN` bytes back.

### `Search.rw()`, `Search.kind()`

`rw()`: registers read and registers written. Capstone lists a compare's first operand as written and the flags as
read by cbz and tbz, and leaves out the destination of adds, so every register operand counts as well: operand 0
written and the others read, or for a load all of them written. `kind()`: `'br'` a direct branch, `'pc'` adr or adrp,
`'ok'` a load or register and flag arithmetic, `None` anything else. A load without a memory operand is a pc-relative
literal load and is `'ok'`.

### `Search.rmw()`

`ld(a)xr wA,[xN]; add|sub wB,wA,#k; st(l)xr wS,wB,[xN]; cbnz wS,<the load>` at `a` gives xN, plus or minus k, the
registers written and the four instructions, or `None`.

### `Search.lock()`

An exclusive spin lock sequence or a test loop entered at `a`, as kind, xN, its instructions, how many come before
`a`, how many from `a` on, registers read and registers written:

```
'acq'  ld(a)xr wA,[xN]; cbz wA,+16; clrex; b <the load>; st(l)xr wS,wK,[xN]; cbnz wS,<the load>
'rel'  clrex; ld(a)xr wA,[xN]; cmp wA,#k; b.ne <the clrex>; st(l)xr wS,wzr,[xN]; cbnz wS,<the load>
       (entered at the load), or stlr wzr,[xN]
'tst'  ldr|ldrb|ldrh wA,[xN{,#imm}]; cmp wA,#k; b.eq|b.ne <the load>
```

### `Search.store()`

str, strb, strh, stur, sturb or sturh of a w register or zr to `[xN{,#imm}]` (no writeback, no register offset) gives
the value register (`None` for zr) and the base register, or `None`.

### `Search.quick()`

A back-edge to T can be reached from T without passing a word of `SPIN_STOP` (only such heads get the full check,
which decodes). str, strb and strh (immediate, unscaled) do not stop it: they may store the same on every pass.

### `Search.region()`

The spin wait with head T (`bes`: the backward branches to T) as a dict, or `None`. A store whose value or address a
pass changes is no part of a region: the paths through it are dropped and the region is found again without them. The
region without stores is taken instead when the wider one does not cover its back-edges.

### `Search.region1()`

An `ldxr` or `ldaxr` word is tried as a read-modify-write first (`rmw()`), then as a lock (`lock()`); a test loop that
is the region's own is not a lock. Every path from T to a back-edge is walked: no inner cycle, and the exclusive adds
on each address (a register between two writes to it) sum to 0; a lock is taken at most once and released after it.
No state is carried from one pass to the next: nothing live at T is written in R. A region of stores with nothing
polled is none.

## `tools/streamline_pin.py`

NVIDIA Streamline's runtime for DLSS Frame Generation (`runtime/vk/vk_dlssg.c`): `tools/make_launcher.py` and
`tools/make_kit.py` put it in the release's or the kit's `bin/dlssg/` beside `nx2navi_dlssg.dll`
(`tools/build_dlssg.py`), and `tools/make_game.py` `place_dlssg()` copies the whole set beside the game.

The files are NVIDIA's own builds from the Streamline SDK release at a pinned version, unmodified: the SDK archive is
downloaded once, checked against its size and SHA-256, and only the files the game loads are kept, each checked
against its own SHA-256. The `sl.*.dll` files are under Streamline's MIT licence; `nvngx_dlssg.dll` is under the NVIDIA
RTX SDKs License, which licenses it only for use on systems with NVIDIA GPUs. The notice written beside them names the
version and each file's SHA-256.

- `DLL`: this repository's `runtime/dlssg/`, built by `tools/build_dlssg.py`.
- `FILES`: the set `DLSSG_FILES` of `tools/make_game.py` names: without all of it the game leaves DLSS Frame
  Generation off.
- `SDK_FILES`: the SDK's files the set takes, by their path in the SDK, with their SHA-256. `TEXTS`: the licence texts
  beside the DLLs, by the SDK file each is copied from.

### `wrong_files()`

The pinned SDK files that are missing from `sdk` or differ from the pin.

### `resolve()`

A folder laid out as the SDK is that holds the pinned files: `sdk` when given (its files must be the pinned ones),
else `cache_dir/streamline-<version>`, filled from the SDK archive when missing (downloaded with `launcher/net.py`:
resumed, checked against the size and SHA-256, and deleted once its files are out).

### `extract()`

The pinned files out of the SDK archive into `dest`, each checked against its SHA-256.

### `place()`

DLSS Frame Generation into `dest` (a release's or the kit's `bin/dlssg/`): `nx2navi_dlssg.dll`, Streamline's
interposer and the plugins it loads, and NGX's `nvngx_dlssg.dll` from the SDK folder `resolve()` returned, the two
licence texts, and a notice naming each file's SHA-256.

## `tools/stutter_map.py`

The stutter map of a play session, from its log (`runtime/diag/stutter_map.c`, `NX2SARIA_STUTTER_MAP=1`; the older
spelling of the name still works), and what a game folder recorded that a pipeline list lacks
([performance.md](../performance.md)).

```
python tools/stutter_map.py report LOG [LOG ...] [--out FILE.md]
python tools/stutter_map.py missing GAME_DIR LIST_FILE
```

`report`: each log's last "stutter map" block (the one written at exit, or the last periodic one when the game did not
exit cleanly) gives the areas ranked by their hitches with the causes, and the pipelines draws met missing that cost
the most; the "stutter:" lines add how the hitches spread over the session. Several logs (one session's rotated
files, or several sessions) are reported one after the other, then summed by area.

`missing`: the pipeline records of GAME_DIR's `pipeline-keys.bin` and the shader variants of its `shader-answers.bin`
that LIST_FILE (`tools/pipeline_list.py merge`) does not hold: what `pipeline_list.py merge NEW LIST_FILE GAME_DIR`
would add, so the next installs build them before play instead of meeting them in it. The files are derived from game
data; the list stays out of the repository.

In `CAUSE` the name takes a hyphen: hand-off has one. The import of `tools/pipeline_list.py` carries `# noqa: E402`,
which stays.

### `read_log()`

The last map block of one log, as head, areas, pipes summary and pipes, and its hitch lines.

## `tools/tracker_api_client.py`

Asks a running game for what a map tracker reads through the tracker API (`tracker_api = 1`,
`runtime/diag/tracker_api.c`), the way a tracker does, and says whether each step works on this build
([tracker-api.md](../tracker-api.md)). Standard library only; it reads, never writes.

```
python tools/tracker_api_client.py [--host 127.0.0.1] [--port 6545] [--watch SECONDS] [--save FOLDER]
```

What it checks, in order:

1. status: the game answers, its title, build and versions.
2. `nx2saria.state`: the area and Link's position as the port itself reads them, and where the heap is: a tracker
   takes heap pointers below 0x1000000000 for none, so with the heap below that it finds no game data.
3. main by its MOD0 header (`search_memory`), first in the range a tracker tries first, then everywhere, and the
   header's two words a tracker checks.
4. Link's position by the tracker's chain (a `.bss` slot of main, the player object, its 37th part) and by the port's
   own chain (`player` of `runtime/diag/debug_tools.c`), which have to agree; the scene's file name from the player
   object.
5. The game-data manager by the tracker's slot, the same object as the port's own (`gmd` of
   `runtime/diag/debug_tools.c`), and the Gold Skulltula token count from its Int flags.
6. `nx2saria.game_data`, as an item tracker reads it: each kind's values by their names' hashes, and with `--save` the
   values compared with the newest `progress.sav` under that folder (save in the game first: right after a save, every
   Bool, Enum and placed object agrees, and of the Ints only the counters that run on, such as play time, differ).

`--watch N` then repeats the position and scene once a second for N seconds and reports the answer times; while Link
moves it prints his heading from the port's rotation (`nx2saria.state`) beside the direction he moves in by the
tracker's chain, so the axis a tracker takes for his heading can be checked.

The offsets are facts of game version 1.1.1's main (build e0f3ccfa); [ocarina-atlas.md](../ocarina-atlas.md) lists
where each comes from.

- `MOD0_PATTERN`: "MOD0" and its three offsets in 1.1.1's main.
- `PLAYER_SLOTS` and the constants after it: a tracker's slots and checks in main (1.1.1). `SKULLTULA_TOKENS`: the
  Int flag of the token count; its entry's tag is 0x20.
- `PORT_PLAYER_MANAGER` and the constants after it: the port's own (`runtime/diag/debug_tools.c`).
- `SAVE_KINDS`: the save's inline kinds this check compares; 32 is Bool64bitKey.
- `MANAGER_VTABLES`: offsets in main of the manager's own vtable and its Bool section's (read in main, not yet live).

### `tracker_player()`, `port_player()`

`tracker_player()`, the tracker's chain: `[main+slot]+0x10` is the player object, its 37th part has x, y, z at +0x1c
(and at +0x28 and +0x30 the direction Link moves in on the ground, with `direction=True`). `port_player()`, as
`player` of `runtime/diag/debug_tools.c`: `[[main+0x4e371d0]+0x480]`, named "Player", its position at +0x31c.

### `int_flag()`

An Int flag of the game data by its hash: the section's `{hash, index}` table probed from `hash % size`.

### `save_values()`

The newest `progress.sav` under the folder (or the folder itself, a file): `{kind: {hash: value}}` for the inline
kinds, the set of placed-object keys, and the file. The save container: magic 0x01020304, the data's start (u32) at
+8, and from +0x28 8-byte `{u32 hash, u32 value}` records grouped by kind; a record of hash 0 starts a kind (its value
is the kind's number; the first kind, Bool, has none). The placed-object keys (kind 32) are an offset to sorted u64
keys that run to the file's end (0: none).

### `live_values()`

An `nx2saria.game_data` answer of an inline kind as `{hash: value}`.

## `tools/ui_textures.py`

Lists and dumps the game's interface textures, for choosing the art the settings menu draws
(`runtime/ui/menu_art.cpp`; [building.md](../building.md)).

```
python tools/ui_textures.py ROMFS [--out build/ui-textures] [--archive Common] [--match REGEX] [--sheet]
```

ROMFS is an extracted RomFS folder. Without `--match` it only lists; with it the matching textures become PNGs under
`--out`, and `--sheet` also writes contact sheets of them (numbered; `sheet.txt` says which number is which). It reads
files the way `runtime/ui/menu_assets.c` does, and decodes ASTC with the runtime's own decoder
(`runtime/gfx/tex_astc.c`, built into `--out`). Everything written is game data and stays under `build/`. It needs
Python 3.14 (`compression.zstd`) and numpy.

`KINDS` is keyed by the second byte of a BNTX format: name, block size, bytes per block.

### `sarc_members()`, `bntx_textures()`

`sarc_members()`: the members of a SARC archive as a name to bytes dictionary. `bntx_textures()`: each texture of a
BNTX file: name, format, size, block height, data offset and size.

### `deswizzle()`

The first level as tightly packed rows, in the Switch 2 GOB layout (`runtime/gfx/tex_deswizzle.c` `tex_bl_copy()`).

### `bc4()`

White with the mask as alpha (`runtime/ui/menu_assets.c` `bc4_decode()`).

### `astc_library()`

The runtime's ASTC decoder as a library, built once into `out`. The compiler's `include/vma` is put on the path:
`tex.h` reaches the Vulkan allocator's header. `tools/build_game.py` `runtime_include_dirs()` gives the runtime's
include folders: `tex.h` includes headers from several.

### `sheets()`

Contact sheets over a mid brown, so white masks and dark art both show.

## `tools/ultrawide_layout.py`

The game's UI layouts for a canvas of another shape than 16:9 (`output_res = 3440x1440` or `1280x800` in
`nx2saria.ini`, or in an existing `nx2navi.ini`), edited in place in the decompressed
`UI/LayoutArchive/Common.Product.*.blarc`. `tools/make_romfs_patches.py --aspect` uses it;
[wide-hud.md](../wide-hud.md) has the feature. The notice at its top is that of what it holds from another project and
stays ([THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md](../../THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)): the pane list and the distances, which are values
only, no code.

The game lays its UI out on a 1920x1080 grid and maps that grid onto the canvas, so on a 21:9 canvas every layout
comes out 34% too wide and on a 16:10 one 11% too tall. Here each layout's root pane is scaled back to 16:9 on the
stretched axis (scale x = 16/9 over the aspect when wider, scale y = the aspect over 16/9 when narrower), backgrounds
that must cover the whole screen are stretched back, and the HUD's parts move out to the screen's corners (wider) or
its top and bottom edges (narrower). The implementation is checked against 1.2.1's `Common.Product.110` and 1.4.3's
`Common.Product.140`: every pane is found by name and every pane field and animation key holds the value the edit
expects before it is written.

- `PANE_MAGICS`: BFLYT pane sections (`pan1`, `pic1`, `txt1`, ...): name at +12 (24 bytes), translate x and y at +44
  and +48, scale x and y at +68 and +72.
- `KEEP_ROOT`: full-screen layouts whose root pane stays as wide as the canvas.
- `MARKER_ROOT`: layouts whose markers the game places over a point in the world (the lock-on arrow, the name of the
  item or character in front of Link, enemy life bars and notices, speech balloons): the code moves a pane under the
  root to the target's projected position in the 1920x1080 grid, which spans the whole canvas. Under a scaled root
  that position would be scaled too, so the marker lands short of its target, toward the middle of the screen (at half
  the distance on 32:9). Their root keeps scale 1 and the scale goes one level down instead, to the panes listed
  there: ones the code does not move and no animation scales, so the markers keep their 16:9 proportions. In
  `AttentionBalloon_00`, `N_PosRoot_00` moves and `N_InOut_00` animates y and alpha; in `AttentionLockOn_00`,
  `N_PosRoot_00` moves and `N_Offset_00` animates y; in `EnemyInfo_00` the parts move and `N_EnemyLifeArea_*` stay
  screen-sized; `BalloonMessage_00` lists none, as its speech balloon part is pooled and its scale goes inside
  (`BALLOON_PART`).
- `BALLOON_PART`: speech balloons are pooled: the game clones `Pa_BalloonMessage_00` into `Pa_BalloonMessage_%02d` at
  run time, and the clones do not keep a scale set on the template part pane, so they drew at full canvas width. The
  scale goes on the part layout's top panes instead (their offset scaled with them, so they sit where a scaled root
  would put them); its animations only key alpha, visibility and `N_Loop_00`'s y. The entries are pane, x and y as
  shipped.
- `FLPA_Y`, `FLTS_SY`: BFLAN animation targets: FLPA (pane SRT) translate y, FLTS (texture SRT) scale y.

### `edits()`

Layout, pane, field and value for the aspect: `k` = 16/9 over the aspect (the root's scale), `w` = how far a
1920-wide layout's edge moves out in its own units (`960 * (1 / k - 1)`). `OFF` moves a pane out of sight. Menus
framed by two side decorations, a header and a background keep the frame at the screen's edges. With `hud_corner` the
HUD goes to the screen's corners instead of the middle 16:9.

### `tall_edits()`

The same for a canvas narrower than 16:9: `k` = the aspect over 16/9 (the root's scale y), `h` = how far a 1080-high
layout's edge moves out in its own units (`540 * (1 / k - 1)`). The HUD goes to the screen's top and bottom edges
instead of the middle 16:9.

### `anim_keys()`

The absolute offsets of each key (frame, value, slope: 12 bytes) of the hermite curves of one pane, animation tag
(`b'FLPA'`, `b'FLTS'`) and target in the BFLAN at `blarc[lo:]`, one list per curve. Found by walking the `pai1`
section, so they hold in any version whose animation has that curve.

### `tall_anim()`

The animation keys that hold positions or zooms on the screen's height, for a canvas narrower than 16:9:

- The minimap's four cut panes slide in on straight lines from 0 at frame 0 to y -360, the bottom of a 16:9 layout
  (hermite keys: the first one's slope is the end point); the end point moves with the screen's edge.
- The large place name of a discovery holds its height (285, then 260) in flat keys; they move to the top edge.
- The dragon's tears map icon zooms its picture to 1.08 and 1.28 in flat keys; on the stretched axis by `1 / k` more.

### `anim_edits()`

Animation, byte offset, the value the game ships and the new value: floats inside BFLAN key tables. With `hud_corner`:
the minimap's slide-in keys end at x 765, the right edge of a 16:9 layout.

### `panes()`, `find_pane()`

`panes()`: pane name to absolute section offset, first occurrence, for the BFLYT at `blarc[lo:hi]`. `find_pane()`:
the pane of that exact name; a version that renamed it stops the script.

### `apply()`

Edits `blarc` (a bytearray) in place for a `width` x `height` canvas of another shape than 16:9 and returns the
members it changed. `hud_corner` (wider canvases): the HUD goes to the screen's corners; a narrower one always has it
at the top and bottom edges. For a layout of `MARKER_ROOT` the root stays at 1 and its listed panes take the scale.

Every edit's value at k = 1 is the value the game ships (a scale of 1, the 16:9 position): it is checked before
writing, so a version with other numbers stops there instead of getting wrong positions. `OFF` moves a pane away and
is not checked.

## `tools/validate_shader_cache.py`

Checks the translated shaders an install's shader cache holds, offline: every `<key>.spv` with its `<key>.res.json`,
from `<cache>/shader-cache.pack` and from the loose files (a loose file stands for its packed copy, as the game reads
them). That sentence is the `--help` description, the string `DESCRIPTION`. [building.md](../building.md) has the
step.

```
python tools\validate_shader_cache.py <install folder>\shader-cache [--every N] [--jobs N] [--no-val] [--out FILE]
```

The game checks little of a cached shader when it loads one (`runtime/gfx/gfx_shader.c` `disk_load_from()`: the
reflection is one JSON object that names its stage, the SPIR-V has its magic and whole instructions) and hands the
module to the driver as it is. A module the driver then compiles wrongly, or crashes on, shows as a missing draw or a
crash in play, far from its cause. This tool puts the question to the whole cache at once:

1. the game's own checks, so the count says how many entries the game would translate again;
2. spirv-val on each module that passes them (`--target-env vulkan1.3`, as `tools/shader_census.py` validates fresh
   translations), unless `--no-val` is given or spirv-val is not found.

The modules are the translator's output; the pipeline code rewrites a copy of each before the driver sees it
(`runtime/vk/vk_pipeline.c` `make_module()`), and that copy is not what is checked here.
`tools/shader_census.py --translate` validates translations made now from the game files; this validates what a
folder already holds, which is also what earlier translators wrote and what an interrupted write left.

`--every N` checks every N-th shader (by key order), for a quick look at a large cache; `--out` writes one line per
failed shader (key, origin, what failed). Exit code 0 when every checked entry passed, 1 otherwise, 2 for a folder
that is not a shader cache. The game may be running: nothing is written to the cache.

`STAGES` are the stage names `runtime/gfx/gfx_shader.c` `reflection_valid()` takes. The import of
`tools/pack_shader_cache.py` carries `# noqa: E402`, which stays.

### `find_spirv_val()`

The path of spirv-val, or `None`: MSYS2 UCRT64's on Windows, else the one on PATH.

### `spirv_problem()`, `reflection_problem()`

`spirv_problem()`: what `runtime/gfx/gfx_shader.c` `disk_load_from()` refuses in a module (`''` when it takes it):
size, magic, and every instruction after the five header words having a word count that ends inside the module
(`spirv_complete()` of that file). `reflection_problem()`: what `reflection_valid()` of that file refuses in a
`.res.json` (`''` when it takes it).

### `Cache`

The `.spv` and `.res.json` entries of a shader cache folder: loose files, then the pack. `Cache.keys()`: every key
with a `.spv` or a `.res.json`, sorted. `Cache.read()`: the data (or `None`) of one entry and where it came from,
`'loose'`, `'pack'` or `''`.

### `pread()`

`os.pread` where the platform has none (Windows): callers hold one descriptor per thread.

### `check_one()`, `group_of()`

`check_one()`: key in hex, origin and problem; the problem is `''` when the entry passed every check asked for.
`group_of()`: a problem with its numbers and ids taken out, so the same fault in many shaders counts as one group.

### `validate()`

Checks the cache at `folder`; returns the number checked and the failures, a list of (key, origin, problem). Each
task opens one descriptor of the pack: positioned reads without a shared file position.

## `tools/vulkan_pin.py`

The Vulkan headers the runtime is compiled against where the system's are older
([building.md](../building.md), [README-BuildKit.md](../README-BuildKit.md)).

```
python tools/vulkan_pin.py [--dest toolchain/vulkan-headers]     fetch and unpack the pinned headers
```

The runtime names structures and constants of Vulkan extensions as they come out (`runtime/vk/vk_device.c` asks for
VK_KHR_pipeline_binary, VK_KHR_dynamic_rendering_local_read and VK_KHR_shader_maximal_reconvergence when the driver
has them), so it needs headers at least as new as the ones it was written against. On Windows MSYS2 supplies them. A
build with the system's own compiler and headers (the kit of `tools/make_appimage.py`, a source checkout) met
distributions whose packaged headers are years older: every file that names a newer extension then fails to compile.
Such a build takes the headers from here instead: the release the others use, from Khronos's Vulkan-Headers
repository, checked against its SHA-256.

Only the C headers are kept (`include/vulkan/*.h` and `include/vk_video/*.h`, which `vulkan_core.h` includes) with the
repository's licence note. The headers are Apache-2.0 OR MIT ([THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md](../../THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)).
Standard library only: the build kit's Python runs this.

- `HEADER_VERSION` (363): `VK_HEADER_VERSION` of that release; the numbers only grow, across 1.3 and 1.4 alike.
- `SOURCE`: address and SHA-256 of the archive GitHub makes of the release's tag. `TOP`: the archive's top folder.
- `FOLDER`: below a checkout's or a kit's root: `include/` and `LICENSE.md`. `STAMP`: in the folder, the release it
  holds, written last.

### `have()`

True when the folder holds the pinned headers, complete (the stamp is written after the last file).

### `unpack()`

The C headers and the licence note of the release's archive (bytes) into the folder, replacing what is there. A name
the `KEEP` pattern takes has no `..` and no other folder.

### `resolve()`

The folder holding the pinned headers (default `<root>/toolchain/vulkan-headers`), fetched and unpacked there when it
does not hold them yet. `archive`: the release's archive on disk instead of the download; it must be the pinned file
either way. Returns the folder; its `include/` goes on the include path.

### `header_version()`

`VK_HEADER_VERSION` as `vulkan_core.h` defines it in `text`, 0 when it does not.

## `tools/worktree.py`

Git worktrees for tasks: `.worktrees/<tag>` on branch `task/<tag>`, linked to the main checkout's ignored data.
Standard library only. Its `--help` text, with the three commands, is the string `DESCRIPTION`.

A worktree holds tracked files only. The folders of `LINK_DIRS` of the main checkout appear in it as directory
junctions, the files of `LINK_FILES` as hard links. Nothing is copied. `remove` deletes the links first (`os.rmdir`
and `os.unlink` never follow a junction), checks that none is left, and only then lets git delete the worktree.

### `main_checkout()`

The main checkout: the parent of the shared `.git` directory, whichever worktree `start` is in.

### `is_link()`, `drop_link()`

`is_link()`: true for a junction or symlink. `drop_link()` removes one link without touching what it points at. A
real directory or file is left alone.

### `unlink_data()`

Removes the links of a worktree. A file link goes by `os.unlink`: a hard link looks like a plain file, and unlinking
it only drops one name of the data, which survives under the main checkout's name.

## `tools/xlate_smoke.py`

Smoke test for the shader translator, `build/xlate/nx2navi_xlate(.exe)`: no game files needed
([building.md](../building.md)).

```
python tools/xlate_smoke.py [--exe <nx2navi_xlate>]
```

It sends one hand-assembled Maxwell fragment shader (writes the constant colour 1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 1.0 to render target
0) through `--serve`, checks the reply, then sends a malformed line and checks the service survives and still answers.
Two hand-assembled compute shaders follow. One loads a texel from a float image and stores it back: the translator
declares such an image without a format, and its read needs StorageImageReadWithoutFormat. The other reads only the
layer count of texture handle 0x10, for which the translator has no type answer: the texture must be declared as an
array, or the count reads as 1 (the expected texture type is 0x103: SamplerType Texture2D, 3, with Array, 0x100).
Every module is validated with spirv-val.

Last, `pool_check()` sends a fragment shader that queries a texture whose handle it reads from an input, which the
translator reads from the texture pool array: the array must have as many entries as `TexturePoolLength` of
`tools/xlate/Program.cs` and the handle must be clamped to the last of them. A translator older than its sources
(0.10.0 and 0.10.1 shipped one whose pool had 32768 entries) fails it. `tools/make_launcher.py` `check_xlate()` runs
`pool_check()` on the translator it ships.

The words of the hand-assembled code:

- `PT` (`7 << 16`): the predicate "always". `SCHED`: the scheduling control word; the decoder skips it.
- `IMAGE0` (`0x120 // 4`): the first NVN image handle in c[2] (`ImgSlot` of `tools/xlate/Program.cs`): image slot 0.
- The shader program header of `fragment_code()` and `pool_query_code()`: word 0 is SPH type 2, version 3, stage
  fragment; word 18 is OMAP: render target 0, components xyzw.
- `SULD_P`, `SUST_P`: opcode bits 51-63 of SULD.P and SUST.P. `TXQ`: those of TXQ (bound handle). `TXQ_B`: those of
  TXQ.B (handle in a register). `IPA`: IPA, pass mode.

### `surface_p()`

SULD.P or SUST.P, 1D RGBA: the data registers from `data` on, the coordinate register, the image handle (TidB). For
SULD the second destination predicate is PT: none written.

### `image_read_code()`, `layer_count_code()`

`image_read_code()`, compute code: R0-R3 = image 0 at x 0 (SULD.P), stored back there (SUST.P).
`layer_count_code()`, compute code: R0 = the layer count (component 2 of the dimension query) of texture handle 0x10,
stored to image 0. Its TXQ is TexHeaderDimension, mask z, LOD RZ; its SUST.P is 1D R of R0 at x 0.

### `pool_query_code()`

Fragment code: R4 = user attribute 0.x (IPA of a[0x80]), R0 = the width of the texture whose handle is R4 (TXQ.B of
the dimension, mask x), R1-R3 constants; render target 0 = R0-R3.

### `spirv_clamps()`

The lengths of a module's OpTypeArray types and the 32-bit constants its GLSL.std.450 UMin instructions take. It
walks the instructions after the five header words: OpExtInstImport (11) of "GLSL.std.450", OpConstant (43: type, id,
value), OpTypeArray (28: id, element type, length), and OpExtInst (12) of UMin (38).

### `pool_check()`

`None` when the exe declares the texture pool array with as many entries as `TexturePoolLength` of
`tools/xlate/Program.cs` and clamps a pool handle to the last of them; otherwise why not.

## `tools/zstd_compat.py`

The part of Python 3.14's `compression.zstd` the shader tools use, on older Pythons through the zstandard package:
`decompress(data, zstd_dict=None)`, `ZstdDict(raw)` with `.dict_id`, and `get_frame_info(data)` with
`.decompressed_size` (`None` when the frame does not say) and `.dictionary_id` (0 for none). On 3.14 the standard
module itself is used; that import carries `# noqa: F401`, which stays.

`LIMIT` is the largest output a frame without a content size may decompress to.

### `decompress()`

One frame of a known size, the common case, is decompressed in one call: the streaming path is several times slower
on the shader archives' hundreds of megabytes. When that call fails there is more than one frame, and the streaming
path takes one frame after another, as `compression.zstd` decompresses concatenated frames.
